.Preface

The OGC API - Connected Systems Standard is part of the suite of OGC API Standards.

To increase the brevity and readability of this Standard, many OGC document titles are shortened and/or abbreviated. Therefore, in the context of this document, the following phrases are defined.

- “this Standard” shall be interpreted as equivalent to “OGC API - Connected Systems - Part 2: Dynamic Resources Standard.”

- “CS API” or “CS API Standard” shall be interpreted as equivalent to “OGC API - Connected Systems Standard” (including all its parts).

- “OGC API - Features” shall be interpreted as equivalent to “OGC API - Features - Part 1: Core corrigendum.”

- “OGC API - Common” shall be interpreted as equivalent to “OGC API - Common - Part 1: Core.”


[abstract]
== Abstract

link:++https://ogcapi.ogc.org/#standards++[OGC API Standards] define modular API building blocks to spatially enable Web APIs in a consistent way. The link:++https://www.openapis.org++[OpenAPI] specification is used to define the API building blocks.

The OGC API family of Standards is organized by resource type. This Standard specifies the fundamental API building blocks for interacting with Connected Systems and associated resources. A Connected System represents any kind of system that can either directly transmit data via communication networks (being connected to them in a permanent or temporary fashion), or whose data is made available in one form or another via such networks. This definition encompasses systems of all kinds, including in-situ and remote sensors, actuators, fixed and mobile platforms, airborne and space-borne systems, robots and drones, and even humans who collect data or execute specific tasks.

Since many of the resource types defined in this document, including the systems themselves, are also features, the OGC API - Connected Systems Standard is logically written as an extension of {ogcapi-features}.

But beyond features, this Standard is also intended to act as a bridge between static data (geographic and other application domain features) and dynamic data (observations of these features properties, and commands/actuations that change these features properties). To this end, this Standard also describes protocols and formats to transmit dynamic data to/from connected systems through the API. Some of these protocols allow efficient real-time delivery of data while some others are more suited for transmitting data in batch.

In addition to providing its own mechanism for interacting with static and dynamic data, the API allows linking to other APIs from the OGC ecosystem, such as link:++https://ogcapi.ogc.org/geovolumes++[3D GeoVolumes], link:++https://github.com/CesiumGS/3d-tiles/tree/main/specification++[3D Tiles], link:++https://ogcapi.ogc.org/coverages++[Coverages], link:++https://ogcapi.ogc.org/edr++[EDR], link:++https://ogcapi.ogc.org/sensorthings++[SensorThings], link:++https://ogcapi.ogc.org/processes++[Processes], and other Features API instances. Among other things, this linking capability allows one to retrieve more advanced representations of features of interest (3D buildings, etc.) and gridded data (coverages) than the one that would typically be provided through this API.

The API is comprised of multiple parts, each of them being a separate standard. "Part 1 - Feature Resources" defines the feature types and corresponding schemas for some concepts of the {ogc-w3c-ssn}. This part ("Part 2 - Dynamic Data") defines the resources, encodings and protocols that allow efficient exchange of dynamic (time-varying) data related to these features, in a way that is also aligned with SOSA/SSN.


== Security considerations

All security considerations detailed in {ogcapi-cs-1} also apply to this Standard.

== Scope

This Standard defines extensions to {ogcapi-features} for exposing metadata and dynamic data regarding all kinds of observing systems and associated resources. It provides an actionable implementation of concepts defined in the <<SOSA-SSN,Semantic Sensor Network Ontology (SSN)>> and also complies with {ogcapi-common}.

More specifically, Part 2 of the API, specified in this document, implements the SSN concepts allowing exchange of dynamic (possibly real-time) data flowing to and from various types of connected systems (e.g., sensors, actuators, platforms). Several encoding formats are defined in this Standard, including JSON, CSV and binary formats based on the OGC - SWE Common Standard. Additional encodings can be added by extensions.

The following types of resources are defined by Part 2 of the API.

  - *DataStream* resources represent data feeds coming out of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Systems`] and are containers for pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`]. They are used for receiving and ingesting real-time pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] as well as accessing historical pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`]. A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] is a particular case of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:ObservationCollection`] where all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] are coming from the same sensor.

  - *Observation* resources record all information regarding an act of observation, whether it is made by an automated device or a human. In particular, they carry the observation result that is structured according to a well-defined schema. pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] are grouped into pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStreams`].

  - *ControlStream* resources represent data feeds going into pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Systems`] and are containers for pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Commands`]. They are used for receiving and ingesting real-time pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Commands`] as well as accessing historical pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Commands`].

  - *Command* resources represent messages sent to a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] to control the parameters of a feature of interest. In particular, a command includes parameters that are structured according to a well-defined schema.

  - *CommandStatus* resources provide status reports during the execution of a command.

  - *SystemEvent* resources provide information about a system event, such as sensor activation, recalibration, maintenance, etc.

CS API Part 1 defines the feature resources that these dynamic data feeds are associated to, including both the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Systems`] that produce or receive these data feeds, and the features of interest that these data feeds provide information about.

CS API Part 3 defines pub/sub protocol bindings for exchanging dynamic data that can be used jointly with the HTTP API.


== Conformance

This Standard was written to be compliant with the OGC Specification Model – A Standard for Modular Specification (OGC 08-131r3). Extensions of this Standard shall themselves be conformant to the OGC Specification Model.

This Standard defines the following requirements classes.

- <<clause-api-common,style=full%>> defines requirements that are shared by several other requirements classes. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-datastreams,style=full%>> defines requirements for pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-controlstreams,style=full%>> defines requirements for pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-command-feasibility,style=full%>> defines requirements for pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resources. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-system-events,style=full%>> defines requirements for pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-advanced-filtering,style=full%>> defines requirements for additional filters that can be used to query _CS resources_*. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-resource-crd,style=full%>> defines requirements for creating, replacing, and deleting _CS resources_*. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-resource-update,style=full%>> defines requirements for updating _CS resources_*. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-encoding-json,style=full%>> defines requirements for encoding _CS resources_* as JSON. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-encoding-swejson,style=full%>> defines requirements for encoding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources using SWE Common JSON Encoding rules. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-encoding-swetext,style=full%>> defines requirements for encoding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources using SWE Common Text Encoding rules. +
  {empty} +
- <<clause-encoding-swebinary,style=full%>> defines requirements for encoding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources using SWE Common Binary Encoding rules. +
  {empty} +

The standardization target for these requirements classes is an implementation of the Web API.

There is no _Core_ requirements class but an implementation target is expected to implement at least one of the _CS resource_* types and one encoding.

The conformance classes corresponding to these requirements classes are presented in <<annex_ats,Annex A (normative)>>. Conformance with this Standard shall be checked using all the relevant tests specified in Annex A. The framework, concepts, and methodology for testing, and the criteria to be achieved to claim conformance are specified in the OGC Compliance Testing Policies and Procedures and the OGC Compliance Testing web site.

[*] _"CS resources" means "Connected Systems resources" and refers to all resource types defined in this Standard._

== Terms and definitions

All terms defined in {ogcapi-common-1}, {ogcapi-features-1} and {ogcapi-features-4} also apply.


=== Application Programming Interface (API)
A formally defined set of types and methods which establish a contract between client code which uses the API
and implementation code which provides the API.


=== Actuation
An Actuation carries out an (Actuation) Procedure to change the state of the world using an Actuator.

[.source]
<<SOSA/SSN, SOSA-SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Actuation++[Actuation Class]>>


=== Actuator
A device that is used by, or implements, an (Actuation) Procedure that changes the state of the world.

[.source]
<<SOSA/SSN, SOSA-SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Actuator++[Actuator Class]>>


=== Command
A Command is a message that is sent to a System to trigger an Actuation. The Command message contains the parameters of the Actuation.


=== Connected Systems
Collections of interrelated systems consisting of information technology (IT) devices, sensors, actuators, platforms, and processes that can seamlessly interact.


=== Control Stream
A Control Stream is a collection of Commands targeted at the same System, and sharing the same controlled properties.


=== Data Stream
A Data Stream (or Datastream) is a collection of Observations acquired by the same System, and sharing the same observed properties.


=== Deployment
Describes the Deployment of one or more Systems for a particular purpose. Deployment may be done on a Platform.

[.source]
<<SOSA-SSN, SOSA/SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/ssn/Deployment++[Deployment Class]>>


=== Feature
Abstraction of real-world phenomena.

[.source]
<<ISO19101, ISO-19101, definition 4.11>>


=== Feature Collection
A set of features from a dataset.

[.source]
<<OGCAPI-Features-1, OGC API - Features, definition 4.1.4>>


=== Feature of Interest
The thing whose property is being estimated or calculated in the course of an Observation to arrive at a Result, or whose property is being manipulated by an Actuator, or which is being sampled or transformed in an act of Sampling.

[.source]
<<SOSA-SSN, SOSA/SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/FeatureOfInterest++[FeatureOfInterest Class]>>


=== Observation
Act of carrying out an (Observation) Procedure to estimate or calculate a value of a property of a Feature of Interest.

[.source]
<<SOSA-SSN, SOSA/SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Observation++[Observation Class]>>


=== Platform
A Platform is an entity that hosts other entities, particularly Sensors, Actuators, Samplers, and other Platforms.

[.source]
<<SOSA-SSN, SOSA/SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Platform++[Platform Class]>>


=== Procedure
A workflow, protocol, plan, algorithm, or computational method specifying how to make an Observation, create a Sample, or make a change to the state of the world (via an Actuator). A Procedure is re-usable, and might be involved in many Observations, Samplings, or Actuations. It explains the steps to be carried out to arrive at reproducible Results.

[.source]
<<SOSA-SSN, SOSA/SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Procedure++[Procedure Class]>>


=== Property
Facet or attribute of an object referenced by a name.

Example	: Abby's car has the color red, where "color red" is a property of the car instance

[.source]
<<ISO19143, ISO-19143>>


=== Sample
Feature which is intended to be representative of a FeatureOfInterest on which Observations may be made.

[.source]
<<SOSA-SSN, SOSA/SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Sample++[Sample Class]>>


=== Sampler
A device that is used by, or implements, a (Sampling) Procedure to create or transform one or more samples.

[.source]
<<SOSA-SSN, SOSA/SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Sampler++[Sampler Class]>>


=== Sampling Feature
Feature representing a subset of a FeatureOfInterest on which properties are observed or controlled. For Observations, Sampling Feature is a synonym of Sample.


=== Sensor
Device, agent (including humans), or software (simulation) involved in, or implementing, a Procedure. Sensors respond to a Stimulus, e.g., a change in the environment, or Input data composed from the Results of prior Observations, and generate a Result. Sensors can be hosted by Platforms.

[.source]
<<SOSA-SSN, SOSA/SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Sensor++[Sensor Class]>>


=== System
System is a unit of abstraction for pieces of infrastructure that implement Procedures. A System may have components, its subsystems, which are other Systems.

[.source]
<<SOSA-SSN, SOSA/SSN, link:++http://www.w3.org/ns/ssn/System++[System Class]>>

== Overview
=== General

All resources defined in Part 1 of this Standard are feature resources, among which the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource. Part 2 (this document) defines additional resource types to describe and interact with the dynamic data that flows in and out of these systems.

Part 2 of this Standard defines resource types that allow the provision of dynamic data about all kinds of devices, hardware components or processes that can transmit and/or receive data via communication networks (a.k.a. connected systems), including sensors, platforms, robots, human observers, forecast models, computer simulations, etc.

Flows carrying observation and status data coming out of a system are called pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Datastreams`] while flows carrying commands sent to a system are called pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Control Streams`] (note that the direction of the flow mentioned here is relative to the real system, which is different from the direction of the data flows going in and out of the API server).



=== Design Considerations

In this Standard, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Commands`] are purposefully not modelled as pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Features`]. This choice was made to keep a clear separation between the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Features of Interest`] that represent concrete or virtual objects (or things) of interest (and in the vast majority of use cases, real-world objects) and the other concepts that are used to encapsulate dynamic data related to these features:

- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] carry the result of the estimation of one or more feature properties, at a given time (and location); and

- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Commands`] carry the desired value of one or more feature properties, at a given time.


Likewise, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStreams`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStreams`] are not modelled as features, as they are simply containers for pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Commands`], respectively. More specifically, they are particular cases of homogeneous collections that are associated to a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] (see <<clause-datastreams;and!clause-controlstreams>>).



=== Resource Types

As indicated above, while part 1 of this Standard focused on defining "static" feature types, part 2 defines additional resources to deal with dynamic data associated to these features.

<<api-class-diagram>> shows a UML class diagram of all Connected Systems API resources. Resources defined in part 2 are shown with a solid border while resources that were already defined in part 1 are shown with a dashed light gray outline.

[#api-class-diagram,reftext='{figure-caption} {counter:figure-num}']
.Class diagram of API resources
image::figures/FIG001-resource-diagram.png[api-class-diagram, align="center"]

The table below provides a short summary description of these resource types:

[#feature-types,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Overview of resource types defined by this Standard
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Resource Type*    | *Requirements Class*           | *Description*          | *Possible Encodings*
| DataStream         | <<clause-datastreams>>         | Description of datastreams, including observed properties and features of interest.| JSON
| Observation        | <<clause-datastreams>>         | Actual observations, including the result data. | JSON, SWE-JSON, SWE-Text, SWE-Binary
| ControlStream      | <<clause-controlstreams>>      | Description of control channels, including controllable properties and features of interest. | JSON
| Command            | <<clause-controlstreams>>      | Actual command messages, including the command parameters data. | JSON, SWE-JSON, SWE-Text, SWE-Binary
| Command Status     | <<clause-controlstreams>>      | Status info about a given command. | JSON
| Command Result     | <<clause-controlstreams>>      | Result of a given command. | JSON
| Feasibility        | <<clause-command-feasibility>> | Feasibility requests, including the command parameters data. | JSON, SWE-JSON, SWE-Text, SWE-Binary
| Feasibility Status | <<clause-command-feasibility>> | Status info about a given feasibility request. | JSON
| Feasibility Result | <<clause-command-feasibility>> | Result of a given feasibility request. | JSON
| System Event       | <<clause-system-events>>       | System events (e.g., deployment, maintenance or replacement events). | JSON
|====

NOTE: The encodings listed in the table are the ones defined in this Standard document but extensions can define additional encodings.



=== API Endpoints

{ogcapi-cs-1} defines the concept of canonical *resources endpoint* and *canonical resource endpoint*. This section provides the canonical endpoints used in CS API Part 2.

==== Canonical Resources Endpoints

The canonical resources endpoints for resource types defined in Part 2 of the CS API Standard are:

- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/observations`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/commands`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/feasibility`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systemEvents`]

==== Canonical Resource Endpoints

The canonical URL templates to access a single resource defined in Part 2 of the CS API Standard are:

- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{id}`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/observations/{id}`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{id}`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/commands/{id}`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/feasibility/{id}`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systemEvents/{id}`]

[[clause-api-common]]
== Requirements Class "Common"
[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
type:: class
identifier:: /req/api-common
subject:: Web API
inherit:: {part1-specurl}/req/api-common

requirement:: /req/api-common/resources
requirement:: /req/api-common/resource-collection
====

=== Overview

All resource types defined in this Standard must comply with a common set of rules, many of which are inherited from {ogcapi-common}.


=== Non-feature Resources

Resources defined in this Standard are not considered features, but many of the requirements specified in {ogcapi-features-1} and {ogcapi-features-4} still apply.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/api-common/resources

part:: All references to the term "features" or "feature" in the {ogcapi-features-1} and {ogcapi-features-4} Standards SHALL be replaced by the terms "resources" or "resource" when interpreting requirements for OGC API - Connected Systems - Part 2.
====


[[clause-resource-collections]]
=== Resource Collections

Resource collections are exposed in the same way feature collections are in {ogcapi-features-1}, except that the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] is set to a different value.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/api-common/resource-collection

part:: A resource collection SHALL fulfill all requirements from link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_collections_++[Clauses 7.14, 7.15 and 7.16] of {ogcapi-features-1}, except for clauses 7.15.3 (parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`bbox`]) and 7.15.4 (parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`]).

part:: All references to the term “features” or “feature” in these requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.
====


[[clause-datastreams]]
== Requirements Class "Datastreams & Observations"

=== Overview
[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
type:: class
identifier:: /req/datastream
subject:: Web API
inherit:: /req/api-common

requirement:: /req/datastream/sf-ref-from-datastream
requirement:: /req/datastream/foi-ref-from-datastream
requirement:: /req/datastream/canonical-url
requirement:: /req/datastream/resources-endpoint
requirement:: /req/datastream/canonical-endpoint
requirement:: /req/datastream/ref-from-system
requirement:: /req/datastream/ref-from-deployment
requirement:: /req/datastream/collections
requirement:: /req/datastream/schema-op
requirement:: /req/datastream/obs-canonical-url
requirement:: /req/datastream/obs-resources-endpoint
requirement:: /req/datastream/obs-canonical-endpoint
requirement:: /req/datastream/obs-ref-from-datastream
requirement:: /req/datastream/obs-collections
====

The "Datastreams & Observations" requirements class specifies how pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources are provided using the CS API.

A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource represents data produced by a single output of an (observation) system (itself represented by a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] feature in the CS API). The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] can be used to provide access to real-time data only, archived data only, or both. The metadata in the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] description can be used to disambiguate between these cases.

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources implement the _http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/ObservationCollection[ObservationCollection]_ and _http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Observation[Observation]_ concepts defined in the {ogc-w3c-ssn}, respectively.



[[clause-datastream-resource]]
=== DataStream Resource

==== Introduction

In the CS API Standard, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources are a special kind of resource that implements the _http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/ObservationCollection[sosa:ObservationCollection]_ concept, with the following restrictions:

- All observations in a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] are produced by the same pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`]; and
- All observations in a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] share the same observed properties and the same result schema.

This section defines the attributes and associations composing a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource, but the exact way they are encoded in the payload is defined by each encoding. For encodings defined in this document, please see:

- <<clause-json-datastream,DataStream resource encoded in JSON>>

Below is the contextual class diagram of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource:

[#datastream-class,reftext='{figure-caption} {counter:figure-num}']
.DataStream Resource Diagram
image::figures/FIG002-datastream-class.png[api-class-diagram, align="center"]

==== Properties

The following tables describe the attributes and associations of a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource and their mapping to SOSA/SSN.

[#datastream-attributes,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.DataStream Attributes
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*               | *SOSA/SSN Property* | *Definition* | *Data Type* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`name`]               | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`rdfs:label`] | The human readable name of the datastream. | String | Mandatory
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`outputName`]         | - | The name of the system output feeding the datastream. | String | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`description`]        | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`rdfs:comment`] | A human readable description for the datastream. | String | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`type`]               | - | The type of datastream (see <<datastream-types>>). | Enum | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`validTime`]          | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:validTime`] | The validity period of the datastream's description. | TimeExtent | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`]     | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:phenomenonTime`] | The time period spanned by the phenomenon times of all observations in the datastream. | TimeExtent | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`]         | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:resultTime`] | The time period spanned by the result times of all observations in the datastream. | TimeExtent | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`observedProperties`] | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:observedProperty`] | Properties for which the observations in the datastream provide measurements. | List<Property> | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultType`]         | - | The type of result for observations in the datastream (see <<result-types>>). | Enum | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`]               | - | Indicates whether the datastream accepts new observations. | Boolean | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`formats`]            | - | The list of formats that the observations in the datastream can be encoded to. | List<String> | Required
|====

The values for the properties pass:c,q,a,m,p[`observedProperties`], pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`], pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`], pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultType`] SHALL be automatically generated by the server based the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] that are linked to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Datastream`]. If there are no linked Observations the properties SHALL be set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`null`].
The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`observedProperties`] list contains pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Property`] values. In the JSON encoding, each value is represented by a compact object that can include a property's pass:c,q,a,m,p[`definition`] URI, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`label`], and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`description`]; this object is not an embedded pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Property`] resource.
The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`] property is client-controlled state that governs whether the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] accepts new pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources. It does not affect retrieval of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] or its existing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources.

[#datastream-types,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.DataStream Types
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Datastream Type*   | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`status`]            | For datastreams providing status observations of the parent system itself or one of its subsystems.
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`observation`]       | For datastreams providing observations of other features of interest (not the system itself).
|====

[#result-types,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Result Types
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Result Type*    | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`measure`]        | When the result is a single scalar value with a unit of measure
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`vector`]         | When the result is a vector quantity (e.g velocity vector, stress tensor)
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`record`]         | When the result is a record containing only scalar values and/or vectors
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`coverage`]       | When the result is a coverage (any number of dimensions)
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`complex`]        | When the result is a record with nested records and/or arrays
|====

[#datastream-assocs,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.DataStream Associations
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*               | *SOSA/SSN Property* | *Definition* | *Target Content* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`system`]             | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:isObservedBy`] | The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] that is the producer of the datastream. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource (by reference). | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`observations`]       | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:hasMember`] | The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] that are part of the datastream. | A list of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources. | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`procedure`] | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:usedProcedure`] | The procedure used to generate observations in the datastream. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Procedure`] resource. | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`deployment`] | - | The deployment during which the datastream was generated. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Deployment`] resource. | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`samplingFeatures`]   | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:hasFeatureOfInterest`] | The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Sampling Features`] that are the subject of observations in the datastream. | A list of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SamplingFeature`] resources. | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`featuresOfInterest`] | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:hasUltimateFeatureOfInterest`] | The ultimate features of interest that are the subject of observations in the datastream. | A list of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feature`] resources. | Optional
|====

In a JSON resource representation, the optional pass:c,q,a,m,p[`procedure@link`], pass:c,q,a,m,p[`deployment@link`], pass:c,q,a,m,p[`samplingFeature@link`], and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`featureOfInterest@link`] attributes are server-provided associations shared by all observations in the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`]. A client SHALL NOT provide these attributes in a resource CREATE or REPLACE request. The server SHALL include such an attribute in a GET response only when the corresponding association is common to all observations in the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`].

When sampling features and ultimate features of interest are hosted on the same server, they are made accessible through sub-endpoints of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/sf-ref-from-datastream

conditions:: The server implements pass:c,q,a,m,p[`http://www.opengis.net/spec/ogcapi-connectedsystems-1/1.0/req/sampling`]

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-sf-endpoint,Sampling Feature resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{dsId}/samplingFeatures`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Sampling Feature`] resources that are associated to observations in the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`dsId`].
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/foi-ref-from-datastream

conditions::
  - The server provides the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`featuresOfInterest`] association as part of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource representations.
  - The server hosts the features of interest descriptions locally.

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-features-endpoint,Feature resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{dsId}/featuresOfInterest`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feature`] resources that are the ultimate features of interest of observations in the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`dsId`].
====



=== DataStream Canonical URL

The CS API Standard requires that every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource has a canonical URL.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/canonical-url

part:: All pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources exposed by the server SHALL be accessible through their canonical URL of the form pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{id}`] where pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource.

part:: If a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource is retrieved through any other URL than its canonical URL, the server response SHALL include a link to its canonical URL with relation type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`canonical`].
====



[[clause-datastream-resources-endpoint]]
=== DataStream Resources Endpoints

==== Definition

A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] {resources-endpoint} is an endpoint exposing a set of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources that can be further filtered using query parameters.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/resources-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL support the HTTP GET operation at the path associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources endpoint.

part:: The operation SHALL support the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`limit`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_limit++[Clause 7.15.2] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in {ogcapi-features-1} requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: The operation SHALL support the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_limit++[Clause 7.15.4] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in {ogcapi-features-1} requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively. Only pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources that have a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`validTime`] property that intersects the temporal information in the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] parameter SHALL be part of the result set.

part:: A successful execution of the operation SHALL be reported as a response with an HTTP status code 200. The response SHALL only include the <<clause-datastream-resource,DataStream resources>> selected by the request.

part:: Error cases SHALL be reported using HTTP status codes listed in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#http_status_codes++[Clause 7.5.1] of {ogcapi-features-1}.
====

<<clause-datastream-query-params>> defines additional query parameters applicable to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources endpoint.

[[clause-canonical-datastream-rep]]
==== Canonical DataStream Resources Endpoint

The CS API Standard requires that a canonical pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources endpoint, exposing all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources, be made available by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/canonical-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL expose a {datastream-resources-endpoint} at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams`].

part:: The endpoint SHALL expose all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources available on the server.
====

==== Nested DataStream Resources Endpoints

The set of datastreams produced by a specific system is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/ref-from-system

conditions:: The server implements pass:c,q,a,m,p[`http://www.opengis.net/spec/ogcapi-connectedsystems-1/1.0/req/system`]

part:: The server SHALL implement a {datastream-resources-endpoint} at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systems/{sysId}/datastreams`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sysId`].
====

The set of datastreams associated to a specific deployment is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Deployment`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/ref-from-deployment

conditions:: The server implements pass:c,q,a,m,p[`http://www.opengis.net/spec/ogcapi-connectedsystems-1/1.0/req/deployment`]

part:: The server SHALL implement a {datastream-resources-endpoint} at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/deployments/{depId}/datastreams`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Deployment`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources associated to a system that was deployed during the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Deployment`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`depId`], and whose valid time intersects the deployment time period.
====



=== DataStream Collections

Any number of resources collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources can be available at a CS API endpoint. pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] collections are identified with the item type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`].

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources can be grouped into collections according to any arbitrary criteria, as exemplified below:

[example%unnumbered]
====
*Examples of DataStream Collections*

- All data collected by organization X at URL pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/orgx_datastreams`]
- All data collected during a field survey involving multiple sensors at URL pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/campaignX_datastreams`]

_Note that a given datastream can be part of multiple collections at the same time._
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/collections

part:: If the server exposes collections of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources, it SHALL be done as specified in <<clause-resource-collections>>.

part:: The server SHALL identify all resource collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources by setting the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] attribute to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] in the Collection metadata.

part:: For any resource collection with pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`], the HTTP GET operation at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`/collections/{collectionId}/items`] SHALL support the query parameters and response of a {datastream-resources-endpoint}.
====



[[clause-observation-schemas]]
=== Observation Schemas

A different observation schema is needed for each individual datastream because the exact content of the observations result changes according to result type and the properties being observed. Moreover, multiple observation formats can be offered for any given datastream, and the schema is typically expressed differently for each format.

Thus, for each pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource, the CS API provides a way for the server to communicate an observation schema (_not necessarily a JSON schema_) for each supported observation format. The exact content of a schema resource is defined by each encoding. See section <<clause-json-obsschema>> for example schemas used for observations encoded using the default JSON format.

Extensions to this Standard can define additional representation formats for observations. Such format extensions must clearly define the mapping between elements of the representation format and the <<clause-observation-resource,Observation resource>> defined in the next clause.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/schema-op

[.component,class=part]
--
For every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource exposed at the CS API endpoint, the server SHALL support the HTTP GET operation at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{id}/schema`].
--

[.component,class=part]
--
The operation SHALL support the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`obsFormat`] with the following characteristics (using an OpenAPI 3.0 fragment):

pass:c,q,a,m,p[```]yaml
name: obsFormat
in: query
description: Media type of the desired observation format
required: true
schema:
  type: string
pass:c,q,a,m,p[```]
--

[.component,class=part]
--
A successful execution of the operation SHALL be reported as a response with an HTTP status code 200. The server SHALL return a single schema corresponding to the format identified by the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`obsFormat`] parameter.
--

[.component,class=part]
--
Error cases SHALL be reported using HTTP status codes listed in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#http_status_codes++[Clause 7.5.1] of {ogcapi-features-1}.
--
====

[example%unnumbered]
=================
*Example*

If a datastream with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{id}`] reports the following supported observation formats:

- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`application/json`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`application/swe+csv`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`application/swe+binary`]

The schema for each of these formats is obtained with the following requests, respectively:

- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`\https://{api_root}/datastreams/{id}/schema?obsFormat=application/json`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`\https://{api_root}/datastreams/{id}/schema?obsFormat=application/swe%2Bcsv`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`\https://{api_root}/datastreams/{id}/schema?obsFormat=application/swe%2Bbinary`]

Note that the media type in the request has to be properly URL encoded, leading to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`%2B`] in place of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`+`] character.
=================



[[clause-observation-resource]]
=== Observation Resource

==== Introduction

In the CS API Standard, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources are a special kind of resource that implements the _http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Observation[sosa:Observation]_ concept.

In the CS API, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources are always associated to a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] (e.g., a type of link:++https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-ssn-ext/#sosa:ObservationCollection++[_ObservationCollection_]). Some properties of the observation (e.g., link to the observing system, observed properties) can thus be omitted as they are provided at the datastream level.

In addition, the CS API does not restrict pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources to have a single observed property. It is thus possible to package the observation result of several properties in a single resource.

This section defines the attributes and associations composing a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource, but the exact way they are encoded in the payload is defined by each encoding. For encodings defined in this document, please see:

- <<clause-json-observation,Observation resource encoded in JSON>>

Below is the contextual class diagram of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource:

[#observation-class,reftext='{figure-caption} {counter:figure-num}']
.Observation Resource Diagram
image::figures/FIG003-observation-class.png[api-class-diagram, align="center"]

==== Properties

The following tables describe the attributes and associations of an pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource and their mapping to SOSA/SSN.

[#observation-attributes,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Observation Attributes
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*              | *SOSA/SSN Property* | *Definition* | *Data Type* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`]    | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:phenomenonTime`] | The time the observed property value applies to the feature of interest. | DateTime | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`]        | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:resultTime`] | The time the result value was obtained. | DateTime | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`parameters`]        | - | Observation parameters, providing information about how the procedure was used to produce this specific observation. | Any | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`result`]            | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:hasResult`] | Observation result, carrying the estimated values of the observed properties. | Any | Required
|====

NOTE: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`] can be in the far past (e.g., geological or deep space observations) or in the future (e.g., weather forecast). The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`], however, can never be in the future.

[#observation-assocs,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Observation Associations
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*             | *SOSA/SSN Property* | *Definition* | *Target Content* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datastream`]       | - | The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] that the observation is part of. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource. | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`samplingFeature`]  | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:hasFeatureOfInterest`] | The sampling feature that is the subject of the observation. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SamplingFeature`] resource. | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`procedure`]        | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:usedProcedure`] | The procedure that was used to make the observation | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Procedure`] resource. | Optional
|====



=== Observation Canonical URL

The CS API Standard requires that every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource has a canonical URL.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/obs-canonical-url

part:: All pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources exposed by the server SHALL be accessible through their canonical URL of the form pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/observations/{id}`] where pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource.

part:: If a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource is retrieved through any other URL than its canonical URL, the server response SHALL include a link to its canonical URL with relation type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`canonical`].
====



[[clause-observation-resources-endpoint]]
=== Observation Resources Endpoint

==== Definition

An pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] {resources-endpoint} is an endpoint exposing a set of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources that can be further filtered using query parameters.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/obs-resources-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL support the HTTP GET operation at the path associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources endpoint.

part:: The operation SHALL support the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`limit`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_limit++[Clause 7.15.2] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in {ogcapi-features-1} requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: A successful execution of the operation SHALL be reported as a response with an HTTP status code 200. The response SHALL only include the <<clause-observation-resource,Observation resources>> selected by the request.

part:: Error cases SHALL be reported using HTTP status codes listed in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#http_status_codes++[Clause 7.5.1] of {ogcapi-features-1}.
====

[[clause-observations-endpoint]]
==== Canonical Observation Resources Endpoint

The CS API Standard requires that a canonical pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources endpoint, exposing all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources, be made available by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/obs-canonical-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL expose a {observation-resources-endpoint} at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/observations`].

part:: The endpoint SHALL expose all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources available on the server.
====

==== Nested Observation Resources Endpoint

The set of observations produced as part of a specific datastream is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/obs-ref-from-datastream

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-observations-endpoint,Observation resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{dsId}/observations`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources that are part of the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`dsId`].
====


=== Observation Collections

Any number of resources collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources can be available at a CS API endpoint. pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] collections are identified with the item type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`].

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources can be grouped into collections according to any arbitrary criteria, as exemplified below:

[example%unnumbered]
====
*Examples of Observation Collections*

- All observations collected by organization X at URL pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/orgx_obs`]
- All observations collected during a field survey involving multiple sensors at URL pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/campaignX_obs`]

_Note that a given observation can be part of multiple collections at the same time._
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/datastream/obs-collections

part:: If the server exposes collections of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources, it SHALL be done as specified in <<clause-resource-collections>>.

part:: The server SHALL identify all resource collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources by setting the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] attribute to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] in the Collection metadata.

part:: For any resource collection with pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`], the HTTP GET operation at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`/collections/{collectionId}/items`] SHALL support the query parameters and response of a {observation-resources-endpoint}.
====


[[clause-controlstreams]]
== Requirements Class "Control Streams & Commands"

=== Overview
[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
type:: class
identifier:: /req/controlstream
subject:: Web API
inherit:: /req/api-common

requirement:: /req/controlstream/sf-ref-from-controlstream
requirement:: /req/controlstream/foi-ref-from-controlstream
requirement:: /req/controlstream/canonical-url
requirement:: /req/controlstream/resources-endpoint
requirement:: /req/controlstream/canonical-endpoint
requirement:: /req/controlstream/ref-from-system
requirement:: /req/controlstream/ref-from-deployment
requirement:: /req/controlstream/collections
requirement:: /req/controlstream/schema-op
requirement:: /req/controlstream/cmd-canonical-url
requirement:: /req/controlstream/cmd-resources-endpoint
requirement:: /req/controlstream/cmd-canonical-endpoint
requirement:: /req/controlstream/cmd-ref-from-controlstream
requirement:: /req/controlstream/cmd-collections
requirement:: /req/controlstream/status-resources-endpoint
requirement:: /req/controlstream/command-status-endpoint
requirement:: /req/controlstream/result-resources-endpoint
requirement:: /req/controlstream/command-result-endpoint
====

This requirements class specifies how pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`], pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`], and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources are provided using the CS API.

A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource represents a control channel that is used to change the state of (or affect) a feature of interest (which can be a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] itself). The state is changed by sending the desired values of certain controllable properties of the feature of interest, but note that the resulting state will not necessarily reflect the exact values requested (if the exact result state must be known, it can be monitored separately using an associated pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource).

A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource represents the real-time stream of command messages sent to the system, as well as all historical commands received through the channel. It can be used to provide access to real-time commands only, archived commands only, or both. The metadata in the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] description can be used to disambiguate between these cases.

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources are available through their parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource, and each command can lead to the creation of one or more status reports (i.e., pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources).

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources implement the _http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/ActuationCollection[ActuationCollection]_ and _http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Actuation[Actuation]_ concepts defined in the {ogc-w3c-ssn}, respectively.



[[clause-controlstream-resource]]
=== ControlStream Resource

==== Introduction

In the CS API Standard, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources are a special kind of resource that implements the _http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/ActuationCollection[sosa:ActuationCollection]_ concept, with the following restrictions:

- All commands in a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] are received by the same pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`]; and
- All commands in a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] share the same controlled properties and the same parameter schema.

When all commands in a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] share a sampling feature or an ultimate feature of interest, that common target can be associated with the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`].

In a JSON resource representation, the optional pass:c,q,a,m,p[`procedure@link`], pass:c,q,a,m,p[`deployment@link`], pass:c,q,a,m,p[`samplingFeature@link`], and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`featureOfInterest@link`] attributes are server-provided associations shared by all commands in the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`]. A client SHALL NOT provide these attributes in a resource CREATE or REPLACE request. The server SHALL include such an attribute in a GET response only when the corresponding association is common to all commands in the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`].

This section defines the attributes and associations composing a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource, but the exact way they are encoded in the payload is defined by each encoding. For encodings defined in this document, please see:

- <<clause-json-controlstream,ControlStream resource encoded in JSON>>

Below is the contextual class diagram of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource:

[#controlstream-class,reftext='{figure-caption} {counter:figure-num}']
.ControlStream Resource Diagram
image::figures/FIG004-controlstream-class.png[api-class-diagram, align="center"]

==== Properties

The following tables describe the attributes and associations of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource and their mapping to SOSA/SSN.

[#controlstream-attributes,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.ControlStream Attributes
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*                 | *SOSA/SSN Property* | *Definition* | *Data Type* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`name`]                 | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`rdfs:label`] | The human readable name of the control stream. | String | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`description`]          | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`rdfs:comment`] | A human readable description for the control stream. | String | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`type`]                 | - | The type of control stream (see <<controlstream-types>>). | Enum | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`inputName`]            | - | The name of the system control input receiving data from the control stream. | String | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`validTime`]            | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:validTime`] | The validity period of the control stream's description. | TimeExtent | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`issueTime`]            | - | The time period spanned by the issue times of all commands in the control stream. | TimeExtent | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`executionTime`]        | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:phenomenonTime`] | The time period spanned by the execution times of all commands in the control stream. | TimeExtent | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`controlledProperties`] | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:actsOnProperty`] | Properties whose value can be changed by commands in the control stream. | List<Property> | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`]                 | - | Indicates whether the control stream currently accepts commands. | Boolean | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`async`]                | - | Indicates whether commands are processed asynchronously in the control stream. | Boolean | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`formats`]              | - | The list of formats that the commands in the control stream can be encoded to. | List<String> | Required
|====

The values for the properties pass:c,q,a,m,p[`controlledProperties`], pass:c,q,a,m,p[`issueTime`], and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`executionTime`] SHALL be automatically generated by the server based on the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Commands`] that are linked to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`]. If there are no linked pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Commands`], the properties SHALL be set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`null`].
The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`controlledProperties`] list contains pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Property`] values. In the JSON encoding, each value is represented by a compact object that can include a property's pass:c,q,a,m,p[`definition`] URI, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`label`], and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`description`]; this object is not an embedded pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Property`] resource.
The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`] property is client-controlled state that governs whether the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] accepts new pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources. It does not affect retrieval of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] or its existing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources. The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`async`] property SHALL be generated by the server and SHALL be treated as read-only in resource write representations.

[#controlstream-types,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.ControlStream Types
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Type*      | *Usage*
| self        | For control streams that affect the parent system itself or one of its subsystems.
| external    | For control streams that affect external features of interest.
|====

[#controlstream-assocs,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.ControlStream Associations
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*               | *SOSA/SSN Property* | *Definition* | *Target Content* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`system`]             | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:madeByActuator`] | The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] that received commands from the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`]. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource. | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`commands`]           | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:hasMember`] | The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Commands`] that were sent to the control channel. | A list of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources. | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`procedure`] | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:usedProcedure`] | The procedure used to process commands received in the control stream. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Procedure`] resource. | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`deployment`] | - | The deployment during which the control stream was used. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Deployment`] resource. | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`samplingFeature`]    | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:hasFeatureOfInterest`] | The common pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Sampling Feature`] targeted by commands in this control stream, if any. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SamplingFeature`] resource. | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`featureOfInterest`]  | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sosa:hasUltimateFeatureOfInterest`] | The common ultimate feature of interest affected by commands in this control stream, if any. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feature`] resource. | Optional
|====

NOTE: In the case of commands/actuations, the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sampling feature`] is used to describe where the effector interacts with the feature of interest (e.g., the vent of an A/C system, the part of a larger system, etc.).

When a common sampling feature or ultimate feature of interest is hosted on the same server, it is made accessible through a sub-endpoint of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/sf-ref-from-controlstream

conditions:: The server implements pass:c,q,a,m,p[`http://www.opengis.net/spec/ogcapi-connectedsystems-1/1.0/req/sampling`]

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-sf-endpoint,Sampling Feature resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{csId}/samplingFeatures`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Sampling Feature`] resource that is the common target of commands in the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`csId`], if any.
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/foi-ref-from-controlstream

conditions::
  - The server provides the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`featureOfInterest`] association as part of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource representations.
  - The server hosts the features of interest descriptions locally.

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-features-endpoint,Feature resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{csId}/featuresOfInterest`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feature`] resource that is the common ultimate feature of interest of commands in the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`csId`], if any.
====



=== ControlStream Canonical URL

The CS API Standard requires that every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource has a canonical URL.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/canonical-url

part:: All pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources exposed by the server SHALL be accessible through their canonical URL of the form pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{id}`] where pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource.

part:: If a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource is retrieved through any other URL than its canonical URL, the server response SHALL include a link to its canonical URL with relation type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`canonical`].
====



[[clause-controlstream-resources-endpoint]]
=== ControlStream Resources Endpoints

==== Definition

A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] {resources-endpoint} is an endpoint exposing a set of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources that can be further filtered using query parameters.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/resources-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL support the HTTP GET operation at the path associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources endpoint.

part:: The operation SHALL support the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`limit`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_limit++[Clause 7.15.2] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in {ogcapi-features-1} requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: The operation SHALL support the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_limit++[Clause 7.15.4] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in {ogcapi-features-1} requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively. Only pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources that have a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`validTime`] property that intersects the temporal information in the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] parameter SHALL be part of the result set.

part:: A successful execution of the operation SHALL be reported as a response with an HTTP status code 200. The response SHALL only include the <<clause-controlstream-resource,ControlStream resources>> selected by the request.

part:: Error cases SHALL be reported using HTTP status codes listed in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#http_status_codes++[Clause 7.5.1] of {ogcapi-features-1}.
====

<<clause-controlstream-query-params>> defines additional query parameters applicable to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources endpoint.

==== Canonical ControlStream Resources Endpoint

The CS API Standard requires that a canonical pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources endpoint, exposing all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources, be made available by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/canonical-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL expose a {controlstream-resources-endpoint} at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams`].

part:: The endpoint SHALL expose all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources available on the server.
====

==== Nested ControlStream Resources Endpoints

The set of control streams available on a specific system is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/ref-from-system

conditions:: The server implements pass:c,q,a,m,p[`http://www.opengis.net/spec/ogcapi-connectedsystems-1/1.0/req/system`]

part:: The server SHALL implement a {controlstream-resources-endpoint} at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systems/{sysId}/controlstreams`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sysId`].
====

The set of control streams associated to a specific deployment can also be made available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Deployment`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/ref-from-deployment

conditions::
  - The server implements pass:c,q,a,m,p[`http://www.opengis.net/spec/ogcapi-connectedsystems-1/1.0/req/deployment`]
  - The server provides the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`controlstreams`] association as part of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Deployment`] resource representations.

part:: The server SHALL implement a {controlstream-resources-endpoint} at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/deployments/{depId}/controlstreams`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Deployment`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources associated to a system that was deployed during the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Deployment`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`depId`], and whose valid time intersects the deployment time period.
====



=== ControlStream Collections

Any number of resources collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources can be available at a CS API endpoint. pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] collections are identified with the item type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`].

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources can be grouped into collections according to any arbitrary criteria, as exemplified below:

[example%unnumbered]
====
*Examples of ControlStream Collections*

- All control streams used to control a fleet of unmanned systems at URL pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/uxs_controlstreams`]
- All control streams used to task agents in a squad at URL pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/squad1_controlstreams`]

_Note that a given control stream can be part of multiple collections at the same time._
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/collections

part:: If the server exposes collections of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources, it SHALL be done as specified in <<clause-resource-collections>>.

part:: The server SHALL identify all resource collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources by setting the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] attribute to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] in the Collection metadata.

part:: For any resource collection with pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`], the HTTP GET operation at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`/collections/{collectionId}/items`] SHALL support the query parameters and response of a {controlstream-resources-endpoint}.
====



[[clause-command-schemas]]
=== Command Schemas

A different command schema is needed for each individual control stream because the exact content of the command parameters changes according to the properties being controlled. Moreover, multiple command formats can be offered for any given control stream, and each format may express the schema in a different manner.

Thus, for each pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource, the CS API provides a way for the server to communicate a command schema (_not necessarily a JSON schema_) for each supported command format. The exact content of a schema resource is defined by each encoding. See section <<clause-json-commandschema>> for example schemas used for commands encoded using the default JSON format.

Extensions to this Standard can define additional representation formats for pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources. Such format extensions must clearly define the mapping between elements of the representation format and the <<clause-command-resource,Command resource>> defined in the next clause.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/schema-op

[.component,class=part]
--
For every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource exposed at the CS API endpoint, the server SHALL support the HTTP GET operation at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{id}/schema`].
--

[.component,class=part]
--
The operation SHALL support the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`cmdFormat`] with the following characteristics (using an OpenAPI 3.0 fragment):

pass:c,q,a,m,p[```]yaml
name: cmdFormat
in: query
description: Media type of the desired command format
required: false
schema:
  type: string
pass:c,q,a,m,p[```]
--

[.component,class=part]
--
A successful execution of the operation SHALL be reported as a response with an HTTP status code 200. The server SHALL return a single schema corresponding to the format identified by the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`cmdFormat`] parameter.
--

[.component,class=part]
--
Error cases SHALL be reported using HTTP status codes listed in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#http_status_codes++[Clause 7.5.1] of {ogcapi-features-1}.
--
====

[example%unnumbered]
=================
*Example*

If a control stream reports the following supported command formats:

- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`application/json`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`application/swe+csv`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`application/swe+binary`]

The schema for each of these formats is obtained with the following requests, respectively:

- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`\https://{api_root}/controlstreams/{id}/schema?cmdFormat=application/json`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`\https://{api_root}/controlstreams/{id}/schema?cmdFormat=application/swe%2Bcsv`]
- pass:c,q,a,m,p[`\https://{api_root}/controlstreams/{id}/schema?cmdFormat=application/swe%2Bbinary`]

Note that the media type in the request has to be properly URL encoded.
=================



[[clause-command-resource]]
=== Command Resource

In the CS API Standard, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources are a special kind of resource that implements a generalization of the _http://www.w3.org/ns/sosa/Actuation[sosa:Actuation]_ concept.

In the CS API, pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources are always associated to a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`]. Some properties of the command (e.g., link to the parent system, controlled properties) can thus be omitted as they are provided at the control stream level.

In addition, the CS API does not restrict pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources to have a single controlled property. It is thus possible to package the desired value of several controlled parameters in a single command, and processing a command can result in actions on several properties at once (e.g., both orientation and FOV of a camera can be modified with a single 'ptz' command).

This section defines the attributes and associations composing a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource, but the exact way they are encoded in the payload is defined by each encoding. For encodings defined in this document, please see:

- <<clause-json-command,Command resource encoded in JSON>>

Below is the contextual class diagram of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource:

[#command-class,reftext='{figure-caption} {counter:figure-num}']
.Command Resource Diagram
image::figures/FIG005-command-class.png[api-class-diagram, align="center"]

==== Properties

The following tables describe the attributes and associations of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource.

[#command-attributes,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Command Attributes
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*               | *Definition* | *Data Type* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`issueTime`]          | The time the command was received by the system. | DateTime | Required*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`executionTime`]      | The time period during which the command was executed. The time period ends when the effect of the command has modified all controlled properties of the feature of interest. | TimeExtent | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sender`]             | Identifier of the user or entity who issued the command | String | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`currentStatus`]      | Current status code of the command (see <<command-status-codes>>). | Enum | Required*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`parameters`]         | The value of the command parameters. | Any | Required
|====

(*) These properties are required when a command is reported by the server but not when creating or updating a command. If provided on creation, they should be ignored by the server.

[#command-assocs,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Command Associations
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Relation Name*     | *Definition* | *Target Content*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`controlstream`]     | The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] that the command is part of. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource.
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`samplingFeature`]   | The feature of interest whose properties are changed by the command. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SamplingFeature`] resource.
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`procedure`]         | The procedure used to process the command. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Procedure`] resource.
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`status`]            | List of status reports related to the command. | A list of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources.
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`result`]            | List of results generated during the execution of the command. | A list of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resources.
|====


=== Command Canonical URL

The CS API Standard requires that every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource has a canonical URL.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/cmd-canonical-url

part:: All pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources exposed by the server SHALL be accessible through their canonical URL of the form pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/commands/{id}`] where pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource.

part:: If a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource is retrieved through any other URL than its canonical URL, the server response SHALL include a link to its canonical URL with relation type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`canonical`].
====



[[clause-command-resources-endpoint]]
=== Command Resources Endpoint

==== Definition

A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] {resources-endpoint} is an endpoint exposing a set of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources that can be further filtered using query parameters.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/cmd-resources-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL support the HTTP GET operation at the path associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources endpoint.

part:: The operation SHALL support the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`limit`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_limit++[Clause 7.15.2] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in {ogcapi-features-1} requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: A successful execution of the operation SHALL be reported as a response with an HTTP status code 200. The response SHALL only include the <<clause-command-resource,Command resources>> selected by the request.

part:: Error cases SHALL be reported using HTTP status codes listed in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#http_status_codes++[Clause 7.5.1] of {ogcapi-features-1}.
====

[[clause-commands-endpoint]]
==== Canonical Command Resources Endpoint

The CS API Standard requires that a canonical pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources endpoint, exposing all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources, be made available by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/cmd-canonical-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL expose a {command-resources-endpoint} at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/commands`].

part:: The endpoint SHALL expose all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources available on the server.
====

==== Nested Command Resources Endpoint

The set of commands received within a specific control stream is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/cmd-ref-from-controlstream

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-commands-endpoint,Command resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{csId}/commands`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources that are part of the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`csId`].
====



=== Command Collections

Any number of resources collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources can be available at a CS API endpoint. pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] collections are identified with the item type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`].

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources can be grouped into collections according to any arbitrary criteria.

[example%unnumbered]
====
*Examples of Command Collections*

- All commands received from user A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/userA_commands`] (would likely be visible only to this user)
- All commands that targeted a specific feature of interest B pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/featureB_commands`]

_Note that a given command can be part of multiple collections at the same time._
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/cmd-collections

part:: If the server exposes collections of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources, it SHALL be done as specified in <<clause-resource-collections>>.

part:: The server SHALL identify all resource collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources by setting the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] attribute to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] in the Collection metadata.

part:: For any resource collection with pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`], the HTTP GET operation at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`/collections/{collectionId}/items`] SHALL support the query parameters and response of a {command-resources-endpoint}.
====



[[clause-commandstatus-resource]]
=== CommandStatus Resource

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources represent a status report describing the status/progress of a command at a given point in time.

When commands are processed synchronously, a single status report is provided in the HTTP response. The status can be either pass:c,q,a,m,p[`COMPLETED`],pass:c,q,a,m,p[`REJECTED`] or pass:c,q,a,m,p[`FAILED`].

When commands are processed asynchronously, several status reports can be issued for any given command. They are used to report early acceptance/rejection of the command, scheduling and execution steps as well as failure and cancellations. It is recommended for a server to generate appropriate status reports to report incremental progress of long running commands.
