This section defines the attributes and associations composing a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resource, but the exact way they are encoded in the payload is defined by each encoding. For encodings defined in this document, please see:

- <<clause-json-commandstatus,Command Status resource encoded in JSON>>

Below is the contextual class diagram of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resource:

[#commandstatus-class,reftext='{figure-caption} {counter:figure-num}']
.Command Status Resource Diagram
image::figures/FIG006-commandstatus-class.png[api-class-diagram, align="center"]

==== Properties

The following tables describe the attributes and associations of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resource.

[#commandstatus-attributes,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Command Status Attributes
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*                | *Definition* | *Data Type* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`reportTime`]          | The time when the status report was generated. | DateTime | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`statusCode`]          | Code describing the state of the command (see <<command-status-codes>>). | Enum | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`percentCompletion`]   | Estimated progress as a percentage of total progress. | Number | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`executionTime`]       | The time period during which the command was or will be executed. This can either represent the estimated or actual execution time period depending on the associated status code (see status code in <<command-status-codes>>). The time period ends when the effect of the command has modified all controlled properties of the feature of interest. | TimeExtent | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`message`]             | A human readable status message. | String | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`result`]              | The result of the command associated to the progress report (this can be a partial result). | List of CommandResult | Optional
|====

[#command-status-codes,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Command Status Codes
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Code*      | *Usage*
| PENDING     | The command is pending, meaning it has been received by the system but no decision to accept or reject it has been made.
| ACCEPTED    | The command was accepted by the receiving system. This usually means that the command has passed the first validation steps, but it can still be rejected or fail later during execution.
| REJECTED    | The command was rejected by the receiving system. It won't be executed at all and the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`message`] property provides the reason for the rejection. This is a final state. No further status updates will be sent.
| SCHEDULED   | The command was validated and effectively scheduled by the receiving system. When this status code is used, the scheduled execution time must be provided.
| UPDATED     | An update to the command was received and accepted. This code must be used if the system supports task updates.
| CANCELED    | The command was canceled by an authorized user. This code must be used if the system supports user driven task cancellations. The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`REJECTED`] state should be used instead if the command was canceled by the receiving system. This is a final state. No further status updates will be sent.
| EXECUTING   | The command is currently being executed by the receiving system. The status message can provide more information about the current progress. A system can send several status updates with this code but different time stamps to report progress incrementally. In particular, the progress percentage and the end of the (estimated) execution time period can be refined in each update.
| COMPLETED   | The command has completed after a successful execution. The actual execution time must be provided. This is a final state. No further status updates will be sent.
| FAILED      | The command has failed during execution. The error and/or status message provides the reason for failure. This is a final state. No further status updates will be sent.
|====

[#commandstatus-assocs,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Command Status Associations
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*              | *Definition* | *Target Content*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`command`]           | The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] that this status report relates to. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource.
|====



[[clause-commandstatus-resources-endpoint]]
=== CommandStatus Resources Endpoint

==== Definition

A Command Status {resources-endpoint} is an endpoint exposing a set of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources that can be further filtered using query parameters.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/status-resources-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL support the HTTP GET operation at the path associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources endpoint.

part:: The operation SHALL support the parameters pass:c,q,a,m,p[`limit`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_limit++[Clause 7.15.2] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in {ogcapi-features-1} requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: A successful execution of the operation SHALL be reported as a response with an HTTP status code 200. The response SHALL only include the <<clause-commandstatus-resource,CommandStatus resources>> selected by the request.

part:: Error cases SHALL be reported using HTTP status codes listed in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#http_status_codes++[Clause 7.5.1] of {ogcapi-features-1}.
====

==== Status Endpoint

The set of status reports associated to a given command is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/command-status-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-commandstatus-resources-endpoint,Command Status resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/commands/{cmdId}/status`] for every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources that are related to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource with local ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`cmdId`].
====



[[clause-commandresult-resource]]
=== CommandResult Resource

Certain types of commands lead to the production of data (e.g., tasking a system for data collection, triggering a process such as a simulation run, etc.).

Different types of result can be attached to a command status report:

- Reference to one or more datastreams containing the result data, each with an optional time range;
- Reference to one or more individual observations containing the result data;
- Reference to a collection of observations containing the result data; and
- Inline result data encoded as described by the control stream result schema (can be one or more records).

NOTE: In the case of a command result provided inline, the result data may or may not be recorded separately in a datastream.

NOTE: For commands executed synchronously, the result can be provided as part of the status report returned in the HTTP response.

The following examples describe how command results are used in various use cases:

[example%unnumbered]
====
*Example 1: Chemical plume simulation* +
A command is used to trigger a new run of a chemical plume dispersion model with certain parameters. The output of the model is a time series of observations where each observation provides the location of all particles for a given time (phenomenonTime). Each processed command leads to the creation of a new datastream that will contain all observations resulting for the model run. When the run is completed, a last progress report is provided with a reference to the datastream.

*Example 2: UAV video footage task* +
A command is used to task a UAV to collect video data while orbiting around a building. The output is a set of many observations (i.e., video frames) that are appended to the existing video datastream of the UAV. When the task is completed, a last status report is provided with a reference to the video datastream with a time range selecting the portion of the video stream that is relevant to the task.

*Example 3: UAV picture task* +
A command is used to task a UAV to collect an image at a specific location. The output is a single observation that is appended to the existing image datastream of the UAV. When the task is completed, a last progress report is provided with a reference to the image observation.

*Example 4: Satellite imagery acquisition* +
A command is used to task an earth observation satellite to collect imagery to cover a given geographic area (i.e., coverage request). The output is a set of one or more image observations that are appended to the existing image datastream of the EO sensor. When the task is completed, a last status report is provided with references to all the collected image observations.

*Example 5: System state retrieval* +
A command is used to query the state of a system. The result of the query is provided inline in the status report (potentially synchronously if the state data can be retrieved quickly).

*Example 6: On-demand processing* +
A command is used to trigger a simple on-demand process that computes temporal averages of parameters in a datastream over a certain time period. The output of the process is provided inline in the status report (potentially synchronously if the computation can be done quickly).
====

This section defines the attributes and associations composing a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resource, but the exact way they are encoded in the payload is defined by each encoding. For encodings defined in this document, please see:

- <<clause-json-commandresult,Command Result resource encoded in JSON>>

Below is the contextual class diagram of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resource:

[#commandresult-class,reftext='{figure-caption} {counter:figure-num}']
.Command Result Resource Diagram
image::figures/FIG007-commandresult-class.png[api-class-diagram, align="center"]

==== Properties

The following tables describe the attributes and associations of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resource. At least one of the properties must be provided.

[#commandresult-attributes,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Command Result Attributes
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*              | *Definition* | *Data Type* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`data`]              | The result data provided inline (encoded according to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] result schema). | Any | Optional
|====

[#commandresult-assocs,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Command Result Associations
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*              | *Definition* | *Target Content*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`observation`]       | An observation resulting from the execution of the command | A list of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources (by reference).
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datastream`]        | A datastream containing observations resulting from the execution of the command | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource (by reference).
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`external`]          | An external dataset containing the results of the command. | Any resource (by reference).
|====



[[clause-commandresult-resources-endpoint]]
=== CommandResult Resources Endpoint

==== Definition

A Command Result {resources-endpoint} is an endpoint exposing a set of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resources that can be further filtered using query parameters.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/result-resources-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL support the HTTP GET operation at the path associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resources endpoint.

part:: The operation SHALL support the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`limit`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_limit++[Clause 7.15.2] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in {ogcapi-features-1} requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: A successful execution of the operation SHALL be reported as a response with an HTTP status code 200. The response SHALL only include the <<clause-commandresult-resource,CommandResult resources>> selected by the request.

part:: Error cases SHALL be reported using HTTP status codes listed in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#http_status_codes++[Clause 7.5.1] of {ogcapi-features-1}.
====

==== Result Endpoint

The set of result items associated to a given command is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/controlstream/command-result-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-commandresult-resources-endpoint,Command Result resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/commands/{cmdId}/result`] for every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource that can be associated to a result.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resources that are related to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource with local ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`cmdId`].
====



[[clause-command-feasibility]]
== Requirements Class "Command Feasibility"

=== Overview
[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
type:: class
identifier:: /req/feasibility
subject:: Web API
inherit:: /req/controlstream

requirement:: /req/feasibility/canonical-url
requirement:: /req/feasibility/ref-from-controlstream
requirement:: /req/feasibility/status-endpoint
requirement:: /req/feasibility/result-endpoint
requirement:: /req/feasibility/collections
====

The execution of certain commands is sometimes impossible due to internal or external constraints (e.g., conflict with other scheduled commands, conflict with another user that has exclusive control on the system, etc.).

In order for a client to know if a command/task is feasible without sending the actual command, feasibility channels are supported by the CS API.

In addition to providing a binary (i.e., YES/NO) response to a feasibility request, the CS API also provides a mechanism for returning detailed feasibility analysis information to a client (e.g., provide chances of success, task execution steps, alternatives, etc.).

A feasibility request is initiated by creating a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource on the feasibility channel. The server can then respond synchronously or asynchronously just like for a regular command channel. The parameters used for the feasibility request are the same as the one for the corresponding commands (i.e., both commands and feasibility share the same parameters schema).



[[clause-feasibility-resource]]
=== Feasibility Resource

A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource is a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource created on a control stream feasibility channel (see <<clause-command-resource,style=full%>> for details).

All nested resources available under a regular command resource are also available under the feasibility resource.



=== Feasibility Canonical URL

The CS API Standard requires that every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource has a canonical URL.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/feasibility/canonical-url

part:: All pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resources exposed by the server SHALL be accessible through their canonical URL of the form pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/feasibility/{id}`] where pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource.

part:: If a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource is retrieved through any other URL than its canonical URL, the server response SHALL include a link to its canonical URL with relation type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`canonical`].
====



[[clause-feasibility-endpoint]]
=== Feasibility Resources Endpoint

The set of feasibility requests received for a specific control stream is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/feasibility/ref-from-controlstream

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-feasibility-endpoint,Feasibility resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{csId}/feasibility`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resources that are part of the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`csId`].
====



=== Feasibility Status

Feasibility status is provided using a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resource (see <<clause-commandstatus-resource,style=full%>> for details).

The following table clarifies the meaning of status codes in the case of a feasibility request:

[#feasibility-status-codes,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.Feasibility Status Codes
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Code*      | *Usage*
| PENDING     | The feasibility request is pending, meaning it has been received by the system but no decision to accept or reject it has been made.
| ACCEPTED    | The feasibility request was accepted by the receiving system. This usually means that the request parameters have passed the first validation steps, but it can still be rejected or fail later during the analysis.
| REJECTED    | The feasibility request was rejected by the receiving system. It won't be processed at all and the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`message`] property provides the reason for the rejection. This is a final state. No further status updates will be sent.
| SCHEDULED   | Unused for feasibility requests.
| UPDATED     | Unused for feasibility requests.
| CANCELED    | The feasibility request was canceled by an authorized user. This code must be used if the system supports user driven task cancellations. The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`REJECTED`] state should be used instead if the feasibility analysis was canceled by the receiving system. This is a final state. No further status updates will be sent.
| EXECUTING   | The feasibility request is currently being processed by the receiving system. The status message can provide more information about the current progress. A system can send several status updates with this code but different time stamps to report progress incrementally. In particular, the progress percentage and the end of the (estimated) execution time period can be refined in each update.
| COMPLETED   | The feasibility analysis has completed successfully. The actual execution time must be provided. This is a final state. No further status updates will be sent.
| FAILED      | The feasibility analysis has failed during processing. The error and/or status message provides the reason for failure. This is a final state. No further status updates will be sent.
|====

The set of status reports associated to a given feasibility request is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/feasibility/status-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-commandstatus-resources-endpoint,Command Status resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/feasibility/{feasId}/status`] for every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources that are related to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource with local ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`feasId`].
====



=== Feasibility Result

Feasibility results are provided using pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resources (see <<clause-commandresult-resource,style=full%>> for details).

The results of a feasibility analysis are usually provided inline. The result structure must match the "feasibility result schema" provided by the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource. The "feasibility result schema" is typically different from the "command result schema".

Below are examples of feasibility results for various use cases:

[example%unnumbered]
====
*Example 1: Tasking a UAV to go to a lat/lon location* +
In addition to a binary (yes/no) feasibility response, the result of the feasibility analysis may include the earliest time at which the location could be reached, as well as the expected trajectory.

*Example 2: Tasking a satellite to cover an area with visible imagery* +
The result of the feasibility analysis may include all the attempts needed to have a high enough chance of success to obtain a clear (i.e., cloud free) image of the area. If the area is too large to be covered with a single image, an estimated success rate and completion time could be provided separately for each subdivision of the geographic area.
====

The set of result items associated to a given feasibility request is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/feasibility/result-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL implement a <<clause-commandresult-resources-endpoint,Command Result resources endpoint>> at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/feasibility/{feasId}/result`] for every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resources that are related to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resource with local ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`feasId`].
====



=== Feasibility Collections

Collections of feasibility resources are also supported, but are optional.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/feasibility/collections

part:: If the server exposes collections of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resources, it SHALL be done as specified in <<clause-resource-collections>>.

part:: The server SHALL identify all resource collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] resources by setting the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] attribute to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`] in the Collection metadata.

part:: For any resource collection with pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Feasibility`], the HTTP GET operation at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`/collections/{collectionId}/items`] SHALL support the query parameters and response of a {command-resources-endpoint}.
====


[[clause-system-events]]
== Requirements Class "System Events"
[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
type:: class
identifier:: /req/system-event
subject:: Web API
inherit:: /req/api-common
inherit:: {part1-specurl}/req/system

requirement:: /req/system-event/canonical-url
requirement:: /req/system-event/resources-endpoint
requirement:: /req/system-event/canonical-endpoint
requirement:: /req/system-event/ref-from-system
requirement:: /req/system-event/collections
====

=== Overview

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources are used to capture information about various events and maintenance operations occurring on (observing) systems such as recalibrations, part replacements, software updates, relocations/deployments, operator handoffs, decommissioning, etc.

This section predefines a certain number of event types but the list can be extended further by extensions.



[[clause-systemevent-resource]]
=== SystemEvent Resource

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources are modeled on the SensorML Event class.

This section defines the attributes and associations composing a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resource, but the exact way they are encoded in the payload is defined by each encoding. For encodings defined in this document, please see:

- <<clause-json-systemevents,System Event resource encoded in JSON>>

Below is the contextual class diagram of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resource:

[#system-event-class,reftext='{figure-caption} {counter:figure-num}']
.System Event Diagram
image::figures/FIG008-systemevent-class.png[api-class-diagram, align="center"]

==== Properties

The following tables describe the attributes and associations of a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resource.

[#system-event-attributes,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.System Event Attributes
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Name*               | *Definition* | *Data Type* | *Usage*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`name`]               | The name of the event. | String | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`description`]        | A human readable description for the event. | String | Optional
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`type`]               | The type of event (see <<system-event-types>>). | URI | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`eventTime`]          | The time the event occurred on the system. | TimeExtent | Required
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`message`]            | A human readable message from the operator. | String | Optional
|====

[#system-event-types,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.System Event Types
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Type URI*       | *Usage*
| link:++http://www.opengis.net/def/x-OGC/TBD/Calibration++[]          | System was calibrated or recalibrated.
| link:++http://www.opengis.net/def/x-OGC/TBD/ConfigurationChange++[]  | The configuration was changed.
| link:++http://www.opengis.net/def/x-OGC/TBD/SoftwareUpdate++[]       | The software was updated.
| link:++http://www.opengis.net/def/x-OGC/TBD/PartReplacement++[]      | One or more physical parts were replaced.
| link:++http://www.opengis.net/def/x-OGC/TBD/Relocation++[]           | The system was moved to a different location.
| link:++http://www.opengis.net/def/x-OGC/TBD/Deployment++[]           | The system was deployed.
| link:++http://www.opengis.net/def/x-OGC/TBD/Decommission++[]         | The system was decommissioned.
|====

[#system-event-assocs,reftext='{table-caption} {counter:table-num}']
.System Event Associations
[width="100%",options="header"]
|====
| *Relation Name*    | *Definition* | *Target Content*
| pass:c,q,a,m,p[`system`]           | Link to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] this event relates to. | A single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource.
|====



=== SystemEvent Canonical URL

The CS API Standard requires that every pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resource has a canonical URL.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/system-event/canonical-url

part:: All pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources exposed by the server SHALL be accessible through their canonical URL of the form pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systemEvents/{id}`] where pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resource.

part:: If a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resource is retrieved through any other URL than its canonical URL, the server response SHALL include a link to its canonical URL with relation type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`canonical`].
====



[[clause-systemevent-resources-endpoint]]
=== SystemEvent Resources Endpoints

==== Definition

A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] {resources-endpoint} is an endpoint exposing a set of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources that can be further filtered using query parameters.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/system-event/resources-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL support the HTTP GET operation at the path associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources endpoint.

part:: The operation SHALL support the parameters pass:c,q,a,m,p[`limit`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_limit++[Clause 7.15.2] and link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_datetime++[Clause 7.15.4] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in {ogcapi-features-1} requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: A successful execution of the operation SHALL be reported as a response with an HTTP status code 200. The response SHALL only include the <<clause-systemevent-resource,SystemEvent resources>> selected by the request.

part:: Error cases SHALL be reported using HTTP status codes listed in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#http_status_codes++[Clause 7.5.1] of {ogcapi-features-1}.
====

<<clause-systemevent-query-params>> defines additional query parameters applicable to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources endpoint.

[[clause-canonical-systemevent-rep]]
==== Canonical SystemEvent Resources Endpoint

The CS API Standard requires that a canonical pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources endpoint, exposing all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources, be made available by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/system-event/canonical-endpoint

part:: The server SHALL expose a {systemevent-resources-endpoint} at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systemEvents`].

part:: The endpoint SHALL expose all pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources available on the server.
====

==== Nested SystemEvent Resources Endpoints

The set of events related to a specific system is available at a nested endpoint under the corresponding pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource:

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/system-event/ref-from-system

part:: The server SHALL implement a {systemevent-resources-endpoint} at path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systems/{sysId}/events`] for each available pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource.

part:: The endpoint SHALL only expose the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources associated to the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] with ID pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sysId`].
====



=== SystemEvent Collections

Any number of resources collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources can be available at a CS API endpoint. pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] collections are identified with the item type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`].

pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources can be grouped into collections according to any arbitrary criteria, as exemplified below:

[example%unnumbered]
====
*Examples of SystemEvent Collections*

- All events related to a fleet of UAVs at URL pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/uas_fleet1_events`]
- All events related to a particular mission at URL pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/collections/mission23_events`]

_Note that a given event can be part of multiple collections at the same time._
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/system-event/collections

part:: If the server exposes collections of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources, it SHALL be done as specified in <<clause-resource-collections>>.

part:: The server SHALL identify all resource collections containing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] resources by setting the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] attribute to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`] in the Collection metadata.

part:: For any resource collection with pass:c,q,a,m,p[`itemType`] set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`SystemEvent`], the HTTP GET operation at the path pass:c,q,a,m,p[`/collections/{collectionId}/items`] SHALL support the query parameters and response of a {systemevent-resources-endpoint}.
====

[[clause-advanced-filtering]]
== Requirements Class "Advanced Filtering"
[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
type:: class
identifier:: /req/advanced-filtering
subject:: Web API
inherit:: /req/api-common
inherit:: {part1-specurl}/req/advanced-filtering

requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/datastream-by-phenomenontime
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/datastream-by-resulttime
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/datastream-by-obsprop
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/datastream-by-foi
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/obs-by-phenomenontime
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/obs-by-resulttime
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/obs-by-foi
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/controlstream-by-issuetime
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/controlstream-by-exectime
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/controlstream-by-controlprop
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/controlstream-by-foi
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/cmd-by-issuetime
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/cmd-by-exectime
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/cmd-by-status
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/cmd-by-sender
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/cmd-by-foi
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/status-by-statuscode
requirement:: /req/advanced-filtering/event-by-type
====


=== Overview

This requirements class specifies additional filtering options that may be used to select only a subset of the resources in a collection.

All filters defined in this section are implemented using URL query parameters and are used in addition to the ones defined in other requirements classes. In particular, all parameters defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/DRAFTS/23-001r0.html#_common_resource_query_parameters++[Clause 16.3] of {ogcapi-cs-1} shall also be supported on all resource types.



[[clause-datastream-query-params]]
=== DataStream Query Parameters

The following query parameters are used to filter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources at a {datastream-resources-endpoint}.

==== Phenomenon Time Filter

This filter is used to select datastreams based on their pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`] extent.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/advanced-filtering/datastream-by-phenomenontime

part:: The HTTP GET operation at an {datastream-resources-endpoint} SHALL support a parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`].

part:: The parameter SHALL fulfill the same requirements as the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_datetime++[Clause 7.15.4] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in these requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: Only the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`] property of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources SHALL be used to determine the temporal extent evaluated against the parameter.
====

==== Result Time Filter

This filter is used to select datastreams based on their pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`] extent.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/advanced-filtering/datastream-by-resulttime

part:: The HTTP GET operation at an {datastream-resources-endpoint} SHALL support a parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`].

part:: The parameter SHALL fulfill the same requirements as the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_datetime++[Clause 7.15.4] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in these requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: Only the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`] property of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources SHALL be used to determine the temporal extent evaluated against the parameter.
====

==== Observed Property Filter

This filter is used to select datastreams that include specific observable properties.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/advanced-filtering/datastream-by-obsprop

part:: The HTTP GET operation at a {datastream-resources-endpoint} SHALL support a parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`observedProperty`] of type {filter-idlist-schema}.

part:: Only datastreams that include an observed property that has one of the requested identifiers SHALL be part of the result set.
====

==== Feature of Interest Filter

This filter is used to select datastreams that are associated to specific sampling features or (ultimate) features of interest.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/advanced-filtering/datastream-by-foi

part:: The HTTP GET operation at an {datastream-resources-endpoint} SHALL support a parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`foi`] of type {filter-idlist-schema}.

part:: Only pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources that are associated to a feature of interest that has one of the requested identifiers SHALL be part of the result set.

part:: Both sampling features and domain features of interest SHALL be included in the search.
====



[[clause-observation-query-params]]
=== Observation Query Parameters

The following query parameters are used to filter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources at an {obs-resources-endpoint}.

==== Phenomenon Time Filter

This filter is used to select observations based on their pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`] property.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/advanced-filtering/obs-by-phenomenontime

part:: The HTTP GET operation at an {observation-resources-endpoint} SHALL support a parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`].

part:: The parameter SHALL fulfill the same requirements as the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_datetime++[Clause 7.15.4] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in these requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: Only the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`] property of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources SHALL be used to determine the temporal extent evaluated against the parameter.
====

[example%unnumbered]
====
*Example queries to find pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] by pass:c,q,a,m,p[`phenomenonTime`]*
[width="100%",cols="3,10"]
|===
| closed interval  | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/123/observations?phenomenonTime=2018-02-12T00:00:00Z/2018-03-18T12:31:12Z`]
| open interval | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/123/observations?phenomenonTime=2018-02-12T00:00:00Z/..`]
| special case pass:c,q,a,m,p[`now`] | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/123/observations?phenomenonTime=now`]
|===
====

==== Result Time Filter

This filter is used to select observations based on their pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`] property.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/advanced-filtering/obs-by-resulttime

part:: The HTTP GET operation at an {observation-resources-endpoint} SHALL support a parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`].

part:: The parameter SHALL fulfill the same requirements as the parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`datetime`] defined in link:++https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html#_parameter_datetime++[Clause 7.15.4] of {ogcapi-features-1}. All references to the term “features” or “feature” in these requirements SHALL be replaced by the terms “resources” or “resource”, respectively.

part:: Only the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`] property of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources SHALL be used to determine the temporal extent evaluated against the parameter.

part:: In addition to the possible parameter values defined in {ogcapi-features-1}, the parameter SHALL also support the special value pass:c,q,a,m,p[`latest`]. When this special value is used, only observations with the latest result time SHALL be included in the result set.
====

[example%unnumbered]
====
*Example queries to find pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observations`] by pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultTime`]*
[width="100%",cols="3,10"]
|===
| closed interval  | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/123/observations?resultTime=2018-02-12T00:00:00Z/2018-03-18T12:31:12Z`]
| open interval | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/123/observations?resultTime=2018-02-12T00:00:00Z/..`]
| special case pass:c,q,a,m,p[`latest`] | pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/123/observations?resultTime=latest`]
|===
====

==== Status Filter

This filter is used to select commands based on their pass:c,q,a,m,p[`statusCode`] property.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/advanced-filtering/cmd-by-status

[.component,class=part]
--
The HTTP GET operation at an {command-resources-endpoint} SHALL support a parameter pass:c,q,a,m,p[`statusCode`] with the following characteristics (using an OpenAPI 3.0 fragment):

pass:c,q,a,m,p[```]yaml
name: statusCode
description: |-
  List of command status codes.
  Only command resources whose current status matches one of the provided status codes are selected.
in: query
required: false
schema:
  type: array
  minItems: 1
  items:
    type: string
    enum: ["PENDING", "ACCEPTED", "REJECTED", "SCHEDULED", "UPDATED", "CANCELED", "EXECUTING", "FAILED", "COMPLETED"]
explode: false
pass:c,q,a,m,p[```]
--

[.component,class=part]
--
Only pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources whose current status matches one of the specified status codes SHALL be part of the result set.
--
====

pass:c,q,a,m,p[```]yaml
name: sender
description: |-
  List of sender IDs.
  Only command resources issued by one of the specified senders are selected.
in: query
required: false
schema:
  type: array
  minItems: 1
  items:
    type: string
explode: false
pass:c,q,a,m,p[```]
--

[.component,class=part]
--
Only pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources issued by one of the specified senders SHALL be part of the result set.
--

[[clause-resource-crd]]
== Requirements Class "Create/Replace/Delete"

=== Overview
[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
type:: class
identifier:: /req/create-replace-delete
subject:: Web API
inherit:: link:++http://www.opengis.net/spec/ogcapi-features-4/1.0/req/create-replace-delete++[^]
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/datastream
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/datastream-live
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/datastream-update-schema
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/datastream-delete-cascade
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/observation
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/observation-schema
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/controlstream
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/controlstream-live
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/controlstream-update-schema
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/controlstream-delete-cascade
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/command
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/command-schema
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/command-status
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/command-result
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/feasibility
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/feasibility-status
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/feasibility-result
requirement:: /req/create-replace-delete/system-event
====

The "Create/Replace/Delete" requirements class specifies how instances of the resource types defined in this Standard are created, replaced and deleted via a CS API endpoint.

All resources are created, replaced and deleted using CREATE (HTTP POST), REPLACE (HTTP PUT) and DELETE (HTTP DELETE) operations, respectively, as defined by the {ogcapi-features-4} Standard.

{ogcapi-features-4} uses the terms "resources endpoint" and "resource endpoint" to identify the paths where these operations are supported by the server. The following sections provide these endpoints for each resource type defined by the CS API Standard.



=== DataStreams

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/create-replace-delete/datastream

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL support the CREATE operation at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources endpoints defined by the following URI template:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systems/{sysId}/datastreams`]

part:: The server SHALL support the REPLACE and DELETE operations at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource endpoints defined by the following URI templates:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systems/{sysId}/datastreams/{id}`]
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{id}`]

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sysId`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] is (or will be) associated to. +
       The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource to replace or delete.
====

The following constraints must be implemented by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/create-replace-delete/datastream-live

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: A pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] CREATE or REPLACE request SHALL include the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`] property with a Boolean value. The server SHALL preserve the supplied value and include it in subsequent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource representations until a subsequent REPLACE request changes it.

part:: If the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] has its pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`] property set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`false`], the server SHALL reject an pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] CREATE request at pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{dsId}/observations`] with HTTP status code 409.

part:: If the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] has its pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`] property set to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`true`], the server SHALL process an pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] CREATE request according to the requirements applicable to that request.

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`] property SHALL NOT prevent retrieval of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource or of its existing pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources.
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/create-replace-delete/datastream-update-schema

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL reject a REPLACE request on a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource that modifies the observation schema if the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] already has nested pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources. The server SHALL use HTTP status code 409 to report the error.
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/create-replace-delete/datastream-delete-cascade

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: By default, the server SHALL reject a DELETE request on a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource that has nested pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources. The server SHALL use HTTP status code 409 to report the error.

part:: If the request contains the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`cascade`] parameter, the server SHALL accept the DELETE request and delete the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource as well as all its nested pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources.
====

NOTE: A schema must be provided before observations can be inserted in the datastream. The schema is provided along with the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource itself. Only one schema (for only one format) can be provided by a create operation for a given datastream. However, the server is allowed to automatically convert observations to/from other supported formats, as appropriate. This implies that the server can also automatically generate equivalent schemas for these other formats. Future extensions may define patterns to allow client to define multiple schemas themselves.

NOTE: After a datastream has been created and observations have been associated to it, the server may reject certain updates to the schema (e.g., adding or removing result fields, changing UoM, etc.). Datastream schema evolution will be addressed in more details in a future revision, but the current workaround is to create a new datastream if the schema changes.



=== Observations

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/create-replace-delete/observation

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL support the CREATE operation at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources endpoints defined by the following URI template:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{dsId}/observations`]

part:: The server SHALL support the REPLACE and DELETE operations at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource endpoints defined by the following URI templates:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{dsId}/observations/{id}`]
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/observations/{id}`]

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`dsId`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] is (or will be) associated to. +
       The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource to replace or delete.
====

The following constraints must be implemented by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/create-replace-delete/observation-schema

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL reject an pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] CREATE or REPLACE request with HTTP status code 400 if the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] representation is not valid with respect to the schema provided by the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource.
====

=== Commands

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/create-replace-delete/command

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-controlstreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL support the CREATE operation at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources endpoints defined by the following URI template:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{csId}/commands`]

part:: The server SHALL support the REPLACE and DELETE operations at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource endpoints defined by the following URI templates:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{csId}/commands/{id}`]
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/commands/{id}`]

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`csId`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] is (or will be) associated to. +
       The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource to replace or delete.
====

The following constraints must be implemented by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/create-replace-delete/command-schema

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-controlstreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL reject a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] CREATE or REPLACE request with HTTP status code 400 if the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] representation is not valid with respect to the schema provided by the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource.
====

NOTE: Cancelling a command is different from deleting the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource with an HTTP DELETE request. When a command is cancelled, the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource remain on the server but its status is changed to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CANCELED`] (and of course the command processing should be aborted whenever possible). Command cancellation is implemented by posting a new status report with status code pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CANCELED`] at the command status endpoint. See requirements for pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources endpoints below.

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-controlstreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL support the CREATE operation at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources endpoints defined by the following URI template:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/commands/{cmdId}/status`]

part:: The server SHALL support the REPLACE and DELETE operations at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resource endpoints defined by the following URI template:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/commands/{cmdId}/status/{id}`]

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`cmdId`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] is (or will be) associated to. +
       The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resource to replace or delete.
====

[[clause-resource-update]]
== Requirements Class "Update"

=== Overview
[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
type:: class
identifier:: /req/update
subject:: Web API
inherit:: /req/create-replace-delete
inherit:: link:++http://www.opengis.net/spec/ogcapi-features-4/1.0/req/update++[]
requirement:: /req/update/datastream
requirement:: /req/update/datastream-update-schema
requirement:: /req/update/observation
requirement:: /req/update/observation-schema
requirement:: /req/update/controlstream
requirement:: /req/update/controlstream-update-schema
requirement:: /req/update/command
requirement:: /req/update/command-schema
requirement:: /req/update/command-status
requirement:: /req/update/command-result
requirement:: /req/update/feasibility
requirement:: /req/update/feasibility-status
requirement:: /req/update/feasibility-result
requirement:: /req/update/system-event
====

The "Update" requirements class specifies how instances of the resource types defined in this Standard are updated (i.e., patched) via a CS API endpoint.

All resources are updated using the UPDATE (HTTP PATCH) operation, as defined by the {ogcapi-features-4} Standard.

{ogcapi-features-4} uses the terms "resources endpoint" and "resource endpoint" to identify the paths where these operations are supported by the server. The following sections provide these endpoints for each resource type defined by the CS API Standard.



=== DataStreams

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/update/datastream

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL support the UPDATE operation at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources endpoints defined by the following URI templates:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systems/{sysId}/datastreams/{id}`]
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{id}`]

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sysId`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] is associated to. +
       The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource to update.
====

The following constraints must be implemented by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/update/datastream-update-schema

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL reject an UPDATE request on a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource that modifies the observation schema if the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] already has nested pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resources.
====



=== Observations

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/update/observation

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL support the UPDATE operation at the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource endpoints defined by the following URI templates:
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/datastreams/{dsId}/observations/{id}`]
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/observations/{id}`]

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`dsId`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] is associated to. +
       The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] resource to update.
====

The following constraints must be implemented by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/update/observation-schema

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-datastreams,style=basic%>>

part:: The server SHALL reject an pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] UPDATE request with HTTP status code 400 if the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation`] representation is not valid with respect to the schema provided by the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource.
====



=== Control Streams

[requirement,model=ogc]

[%metadata]

  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/systems/{sysId}/controlstreams/{id}`]
  - pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{api_root}/controlstreams/{id}`]

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`sysId`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`System`] resource the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] is associated to. +
       The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`id`] parameter is the local identifier of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource to update.

The following constraints must be implemented by the server.

[requirement,model=ogc]

part:: The server SHALL reject an UPDATE request on a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource that modifies the command schema if the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] already has nested pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources.
====

== Requirements Classes for Encodings

:json-mediatype: application/json


[[clause-encoding-json]]
=== Requirements Class "JSON Encoding"

==== Overview
[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
type:: class
identifier:: /req/json
subject:: Web API
inherit:: link:++http://www.opengis.net/spec/SWE/3.0/req/json-record-components++[]

requirement:: /req/json/mediatype-read
requirement:: /req/json/mediatype-write
requirement:: /req/json/datastream-schema
requirement:: /req/json/obsschema-schema
requirement:: /req/json/observation-schema
requirement:: /req/json/observation-constraints
requirement:: /req/json/controlstream-schema
requirement:: /req/json/commandschema-schema
requirement:: /req/json/command-schema
requirement:: /req/json/command-constraints
requirement:: /req/json/commandstatus-schema
requirement:: /req/json/commandresult-schema
requirement:: /req/json/commandresult-constraints
requirement:: /req/json/systemevent-schema
====

This requirements class defines general JSON encodings for all resource types defined in part 2.


==== Media Type

The media type used to advertise support for this encoding is pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{json-mediatype}`].

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/mediatype-read

part:: The server SHALL accept resource retrieval (read) requests with media type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{json-mediatype}`] for all resource types whose representation is specified in this requirements class.

part:: The response to such request SHALL be encoded as specified in the clause corresponding to the resource type.
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/mediatype-write

conditions:: The server implements <<clause-resource-crd,style=basic%>>.

part:: The server SHALL accept resource insertion (write) requests with media type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{json-mediatype}`] for all resource types whose representation is specified in this requirements class.

part:: The resource representation provided in the request SHALL be encoded as specified in the clause corresponding to the resource type.
====


[[clause-json-datastream]]
==== DataStream Representation

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/datastream-schema

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-datastream-schema}.

part:: A JSON document supplied in a CREATE or REPLACE request, either as a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource or as an array of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources, SHALL contain only resources valid against the JSON schema {json-datastream-create-schema}.

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`] property SHALL be included with a Boolean value in a JSON pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource supplied in a CREATE or REPLACE request and in every JSON pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resource returned by the server.

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a collection of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`DataStream`] resources SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-datastream-collection-schema}.
====

[[example_json_datastream1]]
.A Datastream in JSON format
=================
This is a simple datastream with a single observed property.

[source%autofit,json]
----
{
  "id": "958tf25kjm2f6",
  "name": "Indoor Thermometer 001 - Living Room Temperature",
  "outputName": "temp",
  "system@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/systems/123",
    "uid": "urn:x-ogc:systems:001"
  },
  "procedure@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/procedures/indoor-temperature",
    "title": "Indoor temperature measurement procedure"
  },
  "deployment@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/deployments/home-thermometer-001",
    "title": "Living room thermometer deployment"
  },
  "featureOfInterest@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/collections/buildings/items/754",
    "title": "My House"
  },
  "samplingFeature@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/samplingFeatures/4478",
    "title": "Thermometer Sampling Point"
  },
  "phenomenonTime": [
    "2020-06-29T14:32:00Z",
    "2022-06-29T19:37:00Z"
  ],
  "resultTime": [
    "2020-06-29T14:32:00Z",
    "2012-06-29T19:37:00Z"
  ],
  "observedProperties": [
    {
      "definition": "http://mmisw.org/ont/cf/parameter/air_temperature",
      "label": "Room Temperature",
      "description": "Ambient air temperature measured inside the room"
    }
  ],
  "resultType": "measure",
  "formats": [
    "application/json",
    "application/swe+json",
    "application/swe+csv",
    "application/x-protobuf"
  ],
  "live": true,
  "links": [
    {
      "rel" : "observations",
      "href" : "https://data.example.org/api/datastreams/958tf25kjm2f6/observations",
      "type" : "application/json"
    }
  ]
}
----
=================



[[clause-json-obsschema]]
==== Observation Schema Representation

When using the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{json-mediatype}`] media type for observations, two separate schemas are provided to further describe the content of the observation pass:c,q,a,m,p[`result`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`parameters`] properties (the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`parameters`] schema is optional). Both schemas are provided as SWE Common data component tree in JSON format.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/obsschema-schema

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Observation Schema`] resource for media type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{json-mediatype}`] SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-observationschema-schema-json}.
====

[[example_json_obsschema]]
.Example Observation Schemas for the JSON format
=================
This is an example schema for scalar observations:

[source%autofit,json]
----
{
  "obsFormat": "application/json",
  "resultSchema": {
    "name": "temp",
    "type": "Quantity",
    "definition": "http://mmisw.org/ont/cf/parameter/air_temperature",
    "label": "Room Temperature",
    "description": "Ambient air temperature measured inside the room",
    "uom": {
      "code": "Cel"
    },
    "nilValues": [
      { "reason": "http://www.opengis.net/def/nil/OGC/0/missing", "value": "NaN" },
      { "reason": "http://www.opengis.net/def/nil/OGC/0/BelowDetectionRange", "value": "-Infinity" },
      { "reason": "http://www.opengis.net/def/nil/OGC/0/AboveDetectionRange", "value": "+Infinity" }
    ]
  }
}
----

This is an example schema for vector observations:

[source%autofit,json]
----
{
  "obsFormat": "application/json",
  "resultSchema": {
    "name": "location",
    "type": "Vector",
    "label": "Platform Location",
    "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/Location",
    "referenceFrame": "http://www.opengis.net/def/crs/EPSG/0/4979",
    "coordinates": [
      {
        "name": "lat",
        "type": "Quantity",
        "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/GeodeticLatitude",
        "axisID": "Lat",
        "label": "Geodetic Latitude",
        "uom": {
          "code": "deg"
        }
      },
      {
        "name": "lon",
        "type": "Quantity",
        "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/Longitude",
        "axisID": "Lon",
        "label": "Longitude",
        "uom": {
          "code": "deg"
        }
      },
      {
        "name": "h",
        "type": "Quantity",
        "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/HeightAboveEllipsoid",
        "axisID": "h",
        "label": "Ellipsoidal Height",
        "uom": {
          "code": "m"
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
----

This third example has an out-of-band PNG image as the result:
[source%autofit,json]
----
{
    "obsFormat": "application/json",
    "resultLink": {
        "mediaType": "image/png"
    }
  }
=================

NOTE: All other observation properties are the same for all datastreams and thus described in the static schema provided in <<clause-json-observation>>.



[[clause-json-observation]]
==== Observation Representation

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/observation-schema

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a single `Observation` resource SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-observation-schema}.

part:: A JSON document supplied in a CREATE or REPLACE request, either as a single `Observation` resource or as an array of `Observation` resources, SHALL contain only resources valid against the JSON schema {json-observation-create-schema}.

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a collection of `Observation` resources SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-observation-collection-schema}.
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/observation-constraints

statement:: The following constraints apply to `Observation` resources:

part:: The value of the `phenomenonTime` and `resultTime` properties SHALL be expressed in the UTC time scale, with an optional time offset.

part:: The value of the `result` property SHALL be encoded according to the schema of the parent `DataStream`. The schema is provided by the `resultSchema` property of the schema resource.

part:: The value of the `parameters` property SHALL be encoded according to the `parametersSchema` of the parent `DataStream`.

part:: See {json-observationschema-schema-json}.
====

[[example_json_obs]]
.Observations in JSON format
=================
This is a simple observation with a scalar observed property, associated to the datastream of the example above.

[source%autofit,json]
----
{
  "id": "1h6pmb3ntfmogfppknk9aefpvs",
  "datastream@id": "958tf25kjm2f6",
  "samplingFeature@id": "living-room-south-wall",
  "procedure@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/procedures/indoor-temperature",
    "title": "Indoor temperature measurement procedure"
  },
  "phenomenonTime": "2021-03-15T04:53:34Z",
  "resultTime": "2021-03-15T04:53:34Z",
  "result": 23.5
}
----

This second observation has a vector result type:

[source%autofit,json]
----
{
  "id": "1125alnna75hafppknk9aefpvs",
  "datastream@id": "1vf8i5ois38u8",
  "phenomenonTime": "2021-03-15T04:53:34Z",
  "resultTime": "2021-03-15T04:53:34Z",
  "result": {
    "lat": -86.5861,
    "lon": 34.7304,
    "alt": 183
  }
}
----

This third observation has a PNG image as its result, encoded inline as a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`data:`] URL.
[source%autofit,json]
----
{
  "id": "fefaig45w46v5186d6w",
  "datastream@id": "f44f85rrt",
  "samplingFeature@id": "55f48g48th",
  "phenomenonTime": "2023-04-03T18:45:23Z",
  "resultTime": "2023-04-03T18:45:23Z",
  "result@link": {
    "href": "data:image/png;base64,dGhlIGltYWdlIGFzIGJhc2U2NAo=",
    "title": "Inline PNG image",
    "type": "image/png"
  }
}
----
=================



[[clause-json-controlstream]]
==== ControlStream Representation

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/controlstream-schema

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-controlstream-schema}.

part:: A JSON document supplied in a CREATE or REPLACE request, either as a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource or as an array of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources, SHALL contain only resources valid against the JSON schema {json-controlstream-create-schema}.

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`live`] property SHALL be included with a Boolean value in a JSON pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource supplied in a CREATE or REPLACE request and in every JSON pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resource returned by the server.

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a collection of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`] resources SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-controlstream-collection-schema}.
====

[[example_json_controlstream1]]
.A Control Stream in JSON format
=================
This is a simple control stream for a camera accepting PTZ commands in JSON format.

[source%autofit,json]
----
{
  "id": "hf62t0dotfd5k",
  "name": "Garage Video Camera 001 - PTZ Control",
  "inputName": "ptz",
  "system@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/systems/4722256",
    "uid": "urn:x-ogc:systems:CAM001",
    "title": "Garage Video Camera 001"
  },
  "procedure@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/procedures/ptz-control",
    "title": "PTZ command processing procedure"
  },
  "deployment@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/deployments/garage-camera-001",
    "title": "Garage camera deployment"
  },
  "featureOfInterest@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/features/garage-entrance",
    "title": "Garage entrance"
  },
  "samplingFeature@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/samplingFeatures/garage-camera-field-of-view",
    "title": "Garage camera field of view"
  },
  "issueTime": [
    "2012-06-29T14:32:34Z",
    "2012-06-29T14:37:34Z"
  ],
  "executionTime": [
    "2012-06-29T14:32:34Z",
    "2012-06-29T14:37:34Z"
  ],
  "controlledProperties": [
    {
      "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/PanAngle",
      "label": "Pan Angle"
    },
    {
      "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/TiltAngle",
      "label": "Tilt Angle"
    },
    {
      "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/ZoomFactor",
      "label": "Zoom Factor"
    }
  ],
  "formats": [
    "application/json"
  ],
  "live": true,
  "async": false,
  "links": [
    {
      "rel": "commands",
      "href": "https://data.example.org/api/controlstreams/hf62t0dotfd5k/commands"
    }
  ]
}
----
=================



[[clause-json-commandschema]]
==== Command Schema Representation

When using the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`application/json`] media type for commands, two separate schemas are provided to further describe the content of the  pass:c,q,a,m,p[`parameters`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`result`] properties (the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`result`] schema is optional). Both schemas are provided as SWE Common data component tree in JSON format.

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/commandschema-schema

part:: The pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command Schema`] resource for media type pass:c,q,a,m,p[`{json-mediatype}`] SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-commandschema-schema-json}.
====

[[example_json_commandschema]]
.Example Command Schemas for the JSON format
=================
This is an example schema for PTZ commands:

[source%autofit,json]
----
{
  "commandFormat": "application/json",
  "parametersSchema": {
    "type": "DataRecord",
    "fields": [
      {
        "name": "pan",
        "type": "Quantity",
        "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/PanAngle",
        "label": "Pan Angle",
        "description": "Rotation of the camera around its vertical axis (i.e., causing the image to translate along its horizontal axis)",
        "uom": {
          "code": "deg"
        }
      },
      {
        "name": "tilt",
        "type": "Quantity",
        "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/PanAngle",
        "label": "Pan Angle",
        "description": "Rotation of the camera around its horizontal axis (i.e., causing the image to translate along its vertical axis)",
        "uom": {
          "code": "deg"
        }
      },
      {
        "name": "zoom",
        "type": "Quantity",
        "definition": "http://sensorml.com/ont/swe/property/ZoomFactor",
        "label": "Zoom Factor",
        "description": "Amount of zoom, 0 being the highest FOV and 100 being the lowest",
        "uom": {
          "code": "%"
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
----
=================

NOTE: All other command properties are the same for all control streams and thus described in the static schema provided in <<clause-json-command>>.



[[clause-json-command]]
==== Command Representation

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/command-schema

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-command-schema}.

part:: A JSON document supplied in a CREATE or REPLACE request, either as a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resource or as an array of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources, SHALL contain only resources valid against the JSON schema {json-command-create-schema}.

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a collection of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-command-collection-schema}.
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/command-constraints

statement:: The following constraints apply to pass:c,q,a,m,p[`Command`] resources:

part:: The value of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`issueTime`] and pass:c,q,a,m,p[`executionTime`] properties SHALL be expressed in the UTC time scale, with an optional time offset.

part:: The value of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`parameters`] property SHALL be encoded according to the schema of the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`]. The schema is provided by the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`parametersSchema`] property of the schema resource.

part:: See {json-commandschema-schema-json}.
====

[[example_json_command]]
.Command in JSON format
=================
This is an example command used to task a PTZ camera, encoded in JSON format:

[source%autofit,json]
----
{
  "id": "1125alnna75hafppknk9aefpvs",
  "controlstream@id": "hf62t0dotfd5k",
  "samplingFeature@id": "garage-camera-field-of-view",
  "procedure@link": {
    "href": "https://data.example.org/api/procedures/ptz-control",
    "title": "PTZ command processing procedure"
  },
  "sender": "user01",
  "issueTime": "2021-03-15T04:53:34.248Z",
  "executionTime": [
    "2021-03-15T04:53:34.543Z",
    "2021-03-15T04:53:36.021Z"
  ],
  "currentStatus": "COMPLETED",
  "parameters": {
    "pan": -10.0,
    "tilt": 23.0,
    "zoom": 0.4
  }
}
----
=================

part:: A JSON document supplied in a CREATE or REPLACE request, either as a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resource or as an array of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources, SHALL contain only resources valid against the JSON schema {json-commandstatus-create-schema}.

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a collection of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandStatus`] resources SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-commandstatus-collection-schema}.

[[clause-json-commandresult]]
==== Command Result Representation

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/commandresult-schema

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resource SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-commandresult-schema}.

part:: A JSON document supplied in a CREATE or REPLACE request, either as a single pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resource or as an array of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resources, SHALL contain only resources valid against the JSON schema {json-commandresult-create-schema}.

part:: A JSON document returned by the server containing a collection of pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resources SHALL be valid against the JSON schema {json-commandresult-collection-schema}.
====

[requirement,model=ogc]
====
[%metadata]
identifier:: /req/json/commandresult-constraints

part:: If a pass:c,q,a,m,p[`CommandResult`] resource includes inline data, the content of the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`data`] property SHALL be encoded according to the schema provided by the parent pass:c,q,a,m,p[`ControlStream`]:

part:: For regular commands, the schema is provided by the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`resultSchema`] property of the schema resource.

part:: For feasibility requests (i.e., commands received on a feasibility channel), the schema is provided by the pass:c,q,a,m,p[`feasibilityResultSchema`] property of the schema resource.

part:: See {json-commandschema-schema-json}.
====
